I suppose at least once or twice most weeks. I suppose so. On a bad night, I'll... I'll start with a plate of leftover funeral-luncheon casserole. And before I know it, I've eaten that, and... half a pan of brownies. Some biscuits. Whatever chicken salad is in the fridge. Just... all in one sitting.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or hobbies that you used to enjoy since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't think so. I haven't really noticed a change with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these nights of eating happen when you're feeling particularly upset or stressed about something, like your friend's passing?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. But... like I said, I don't really know. I just... I just eat. I guess... maybe while I'm doing it. But mostly I just... I don't do anything like that. I just... go to bed.\n\nDoctor: How often do these nights of eating like this happen?\n\nPatient: ...Well. Ever since my friend passed away, it's been... I suppose at least once or twice most weeks. There really hasn't been a month where it just... let up.\n\nDoctor: During these nights, do you typically eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within a couple of hours?\n\nPatient: ...Yes. I suppose so. On a bad night, I'll... I'll start with a plate of leftover funeral-luncheon casserole. And before I know it, I've eaten that, and... half a pan of brownies. Some biscuits. Whatever chicken salad is in the fridge. Just... all in one sitting.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or hobbies that you used to enjoy since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't think so. I still... do my usual things. I haven't really noticed a change with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these nights of eating happen when you're feeling particularly upset or stressed about something, like your friend's passing?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. Maybe. I try not to think about it too much. It just... happens, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these nights of eating happen when you are feeling sad or upset about your friend's passing?\n\nPatient: ...I think you just asked me that. But... like I said, I don't really know. I just... I just eat. I don't really sit there and analyze why I'm doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating like this makes you feel better, even if it's just for a little while?\n\nPatient: ...I'm not sure. I guess... maybe while I'm doing it. But mostly I just... I just feel worse after.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating like this is a way for you to deal with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. It's just... eating. I'm not really trying to deal with anything, I don't think. It just happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel upset or emotional right before one of these eating episodes starts?\n\nPatient: ...I don't really pay attention to that. I just... I don't know. I'm not really tracking how I feel before it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these eating episodes would happen if you weren't feeling upset or emotional about something?\n\nPatient: ...I really couldn't say. Like I said, I don't think about it like that. I don't know if being upset has anything to do with it... I couldn't tell you if it would happen anyway or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these eating episodes are triggered by feeling sad or upset about your friend's passing or anything else?\n\nPatient: ...I'd really rather not talk about my friend. I just... I don't think it's connected like that. I'm not sitting there crying and then going to the kitchen, it's just... I don't know why it happens. I just eat.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

There really hasn't been a month where it just... let up.\n\nDoctor: During these nights, do you typically eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within a couple of hours?\n\nPatient: ...Yes. I suppose so. On a bad night, I'll... I'll start with a plate of leftover funeral-luncheon casserole. And before I know it, I've eaten that, and... half a pan of brownies. Some biscuits. Whatever chicken salad is in the fridge. Just... all in one sitting.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or hobbies that you used to enjoy since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't think so. I still... do my usual things. I haven't really noticed a change with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these nights of eating happen when you're feeling particularly upset or stressed about something, like your friend's passing?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. Maybe. I try not to think about it too much. It just... happens, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these nights of eating happen when you are feeling sad or upset about your friend's passing?\n\nPatient: ...I think you just asked me that. But... like I said, I don't really know. I just... I just eat. I don't really sit there and analyze why I'm doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating like this makes you feel better, even if it's just for a little while?\n\nPatient: ...I'm not sure. I guess... maybe while I'm doing it. But mostly I just... I just feel worse after.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating like this is a way for you to deal with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. It's just... eating. I'm not really trying to deal with anything, I don't think. It just happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel upset or emotional right before one of these eating episodes starts?\n\nPatient: ...I don't really pay attention to that. I just... I don't know. I'm not really tracking how I feel before it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these eating episodes would happen if you weren't feeling upset or emotional about something?\n\nPatient: ...I really couldn't say. Like I said, I don't think about it like that. I don't know if being upset has anything to do with it... I couldn't tell you if it would happen anyway or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these eating episodes are triggered by feeling sad or upset about your friend's passing or anything else?\n\nPatient: ...I'd really rather not talk about my friend. I just... I don't think it's connected like that. I'm not sitting there crying and then going to the kitchen, it's just... I don't know why it happens. I just eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the eating happens because you're trying to comfort yourself or escape from feeling upset?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really look at it like that. There really hasn't been a month where it just... let up.\n\nDoctor: During these nights, do you typically eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within a couple of hours?\n\nPatient: ...Yes. I suppose so. On a bad night, I'll... I'll start with a plate of leftover funeral-luncheon casserole. And before I know it, I've eaten that, and... half a pan of brownies. Some biscuits. I just eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the eating happens because you're trying to comfort yourself or escape from feeling upset?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really look at it like that. a pan of brownies, some biscuits, and whatever chicken salad is in the fridge all in one sitting.\n- key: S050\n  name: Loss_of_Control\n  description: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n  manifestation: Once I take the first bite after the house is quiet, it's like my good sense steps aside, and even while I'm telling myself, 'Belinda, stop now,' my hands keep reaching for more.\n- key: S051\n  name: Rapid_Eating\n  description: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n  manifestation: I eat so fast standing at the kitchen counter that I barely taste the pound cake or the crackers, and sometimes I catch myself swallowing before I've rightly chewed.\n- key: S052\n  name: Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full\n  description: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n  manifestation: I keep going until my stomach is tight and sore under my nightgown and I have to ease myself into the recliner because moving around hurts.\n- key: S054\n  name: Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment\n  description: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n  manifestation: At bridge or church suppers I make a point of taking a modest little plate, then later after everyone has gone home I pull the leftovers out and eat in the kitchen where nobody can see me.\n- key: S055\n  name: Negative_Affect_After_Binge\n  description: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n  manifestation: I came because after these nights of eating late-night eating spells have been happening at least once or twice most weeks, and there has not been a month where they really let up.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I have started turning down some bridge nights and church kitchen duties because I am so worried someone will notice the missing leftovers or ask why I seem ashamed.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about five months, ever since my closest bridge partner and church friend died.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: friend's passing and other issues were resolved?\n\nPatient: I... look, I asked you please not to bring her up. I don't know. I don't know what would happen. If I knew how to make it stop, I wouldn't be sitting here. I just... I really don't have an answer to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating episodes are related to feeling upset or emotional?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know what else to say about this.
